persons or fraction
safety officer for persons or fraction thereof
thereof

(4) Duties of safety officer: The duties of the safety officers shall be to
advise and assist the establishment in fulfillment of its obligations,
statutory or otherwise, concerning the prevention of personal injuries and
maintaining a safe working environment. These duties shall include the
following, namely: -
(a) to advise the concerned departments in planning and organising
measures necessary for the effective control of personal injuries;
(b) to advise on safety aspects in all job studies and to carryout detailed job
safety studies of selected jobs;
(c) to check and evaluate the effectiveness of the action taken or proposed to
be taken to prevent personal injuries;
(d) to advise the purchasing and stores departments in ensuring high quality
and availability of personal protective equipment;
(e) to advise on matters relating to carrying out plant safety inspections;
(f) to carry out plant safety inspections in order to observe the physical

conditions of work and the work practices and procedures followed by
workers and to render advice on measures to be adopted for removing the
unsafe physical conditions and preventing unsafe actions by workers;
(g) to render assistance on matters relating to reporting and investigation of
industrial accidents and occupational diseases;
(h) to investigate all accidents and near-miss incidents;
(i) to render assistance for the cases of industrial diseases contracted and in
respect of dangerous occurrences reportable under Rule 10.
(j) to render assistance on the maintenance of such records as are necessary
relating to accidents, dangerous occurrences and industrial diseases;
(k)to promote the setting up of safety committees and act as adviser and
catalyst to such committees;
(l) to organise, in association with the concerned departments, campaigns,
competitions, contests and other activities which will develop and maintain
the interest of the workers in establishing and maintaining safe conditions
of work and procedure; and
(m) to design and conduct either independently or in collaboration with the
training department, suitable training and educational programmes for the
prevention of personal injuries and for the propagation of safety awareness.
(5) Facilities to be provided to safety officer:- An employer of the
establishment shallprovide each Safety Officer with adequate technical and
secretarial staff and other facilities, equipment and information as are
necessary to enable him to discharge his duties effectively and efficiently.
(6) Safety Officer’s Report: Every Chief Safety officer or Safety officer where
there is no Chief safety officer, working in a Factory as required under this
rule, shall submit through the occupier of his factory, a report in writing
with all relevant details to the Chief Inspector cum Facilitator, in the month
of January and July every year in FORM-XXXI, on the activities/initiatives
taken up during the preceding half year and the progress achieved in the
prescribed pro-forma
(7) Prohibition of performance of other duties:No Safety Officer shall be
required or permitted to do any work which is inconsistent with or
detrimental to the performance of the duties prescribed in sub-rule (3)
CHAPTER-V
HOURS OF WORK AND ANNUAL LEAVE WITH WAGES

24. Daily &Weekly working hours under clause (b) of sub-section (1)
of Section 25.-
(a) No worker or employee shall be required or allowed to work in an
establishment for more than forty-eight hours in any week.
(b) The period of work of a worker or employee shall be so arranged that
inclusive of his /her intervals for rest, shall not spread over for more than
12 hours in a day.
(c) The periods of work of workers or employees shall not exceed six hours
before he has had an interval for rest of at least half an hour.
(d) The working hours in a day may be modified subject to sub-rules (1), (2)
and (3), such that the total number of working hours in a week shall be so
fixed and followed.
Provided that the flexibility in working hours shall be permitted with
the consentof majority of workers or employees.
Provided that the State Government, by notification, exempt any
establishment or class of establishments from the above provisions subject
to specified conditions.
25. Weekly day of rest under sub-section (2) of Section 26.
(1) Subject to Section 2(zzk) of the code, the first day of the week is the
weekly day of rest for each worker in a factory/establishment. No adult
worker shall be required or allowed to work in a factory/establishment on
the weekly day of rest (hereinafter referred to as the said day), unless-
(a) For the purpose of section 26, there shall be posted up in a conspicuous
place outside the office of every establishment a notice showing the weekly
holiday. Where the weekly holiday is not the same day for all persons
employed in the establishment, the notice shall show the holiday allowed
to each relay, or set of persons or individual in Telugu or English
Language.
(b) The State Government may, by notification, exempt employees or class of
employees of any establishment or class of establishments from the
operation of sub-section (1) of section 26 of the Code subject to the
condition given in the notification.
(c) He/she has or will have a holiday for a whole day on one of the three days
immediately before or after the said day, and

(d) The employer of the establishment has, before the said day or the
substituted day under clause (a), whichever is earlier,-
(i) delivered a notice at the office of the Inspector-cum-facilitator of his/her
intention to require the worker to work on the said day and of the day
which is to be substituted, and
(ii) displayed a notice to that effect in the factory/establishment.
Provided that no substitution shall be made which will result in any worker
working formore than ten days consecutively without a holiday for a whole
day.
(2) Notices given under sub-rule (b) may be cancelled by a notice delivered
at the office of the Inspector-cum-facilitator and a notice displayed in the
establishment not later than the day before the said day or the holiday to
be cancelled, whichever is earlier.
(3) Where, in accordance with the provisions of sub-rule (b), any worker
works on the said day and has had a holiday on one of the three days
immediately before it, that said day shall, for the purpose of calculating his
weekly hours of work, be included in the preceding week.

26. Compensatory holidays under sub-section (3) of section 26:
(1) Except in the case of a worker engaged in any work which, for technical
reasons, must be carried on continuously throughout the day, the
compensatory holidays to be allowed under sub-section (3) of section 26 of
the Code shall be so spaced that not more than two (2) compensatory
holidays are given in one week.
(2) The employer of the establishment shall display, on or before the end of
the month in which holidays are lost, a notice in respect of workers allowed
compensatory holidays during the following month and of the dates
thereof, at the place at which the notice of periods of work prescribed
under Section 26 is displayed. Any subsequent change in the notice in
respect of any compensatory holiday shall be made not less than three (3)
days in advance of the date of that holiday.
(3) Any compensatory holiday or holidays to which a worker is entitled shall
be given to him before he is discharged or dismissed and shall not be
reckoned as part of any period of notice required to be given before
discharge or dismissal.

(4) The manager shall, in the prescribed record of attendance, indicate the
days on which the worker was required to work and the days on which he
was allowed the compensatory holidays.
27. Extra wages for overtime under Section 27: -
(1) Where in an establishment,
a) a worker works for more than eight hours in any day as daily wager or,
b) a worker, other than daily wager, for more than forty-eight hours in a
week, as the case may be,
Worker shall in respect of such over-time work be entitled to wages at
the rate of twice his rate of wages and shall be paid at the end of each
wage period.
(2) In calculating over time on any day, a fraction of an hour between 15 to 30
minutes shall be counted as 30 minutes, and in case of more than 30
minutes, it shall be rounded and shall be counted as an hour on an actual
basis.
(3) In calculating the wages or earnings in the case of a worker paid by the
month, the daily wages shall be 1/26th of his monthly wages; and in the
case of any other worker, it shall be the daily wages or earnings, as the
case may be.
(4) Notwithstanding the conditions on hours of work under Rule 24(1), a
worker in an establishment may be required or allowed to work extra hours
with the previous consent of the worker in writing for any of the following
works and circumstances as may be recorded in the said consent.
(b) work in the nature of preparatory or complementary work;
(c) work which is necessarily so intermittent that the intervals during which
they do not work while on duty ordinarily amount to more than the
intervals for rest;
(d) work which, for technical reasons, must be carried on continuously;
(e) engaged in making or supplying articles of prime necessity which must be
made or supplied every day,
(f) engaged in a process which cannot be carried on except during fixed
seasons;

(g) engaged in a process which cannot be carried on except at times
dependent on the irregular action of natural forces;
(h) engaged in an engine room or boiler-houses or in attending to power-plant
or transmission machinery;
(i) engaged in the process on account of the breakdown of machinery;
(j) engaged in the loading or unloading of railway wagons or lorries, or trucks;
(k) exceptional pressure of work and
(l) engaged in any work, which is notified by the central Government in the
Official Gazette as a work of national importance;
(5) Period of overtime work shall be entered in overtime slips in duplicate with
the below particulars, and a copy of which, duly signed by the manager or
by a person duly authorised by him, shall be given to the worker
immediately after completion of the overtime work.
Overtime Slip

Name & Address of the Establishment:

Name & Designation of the Worker

Date of Work & Normal Period of Work

Overtime period and duration in Hours
Work performed on Overtime
Signature of Manager/Person Authorized
(6) The employment of a worker on overtime shall be subject to the following
restrictions with respect to number of hours of overtime work to be worked
by any worker.
(a) No worker shall be required or allowed to work in an establishment for
more than 8 hours on overtime in any day.
(b) No worker shall be required or allowed to work in an establishment for
more than 24 hours on overtime in any week.
(c) No worker shall be required or allowed to work in an establishment for
more than 48 hours on overtime in any month.

(d) No worker shall be required or allowed to work in an establishment for
more than 144 hours on overtime in any quarter.
28. Exemption for overlapping of shifts: Employees working in
establishments specified in the Schedule furnished hereunder shall
be exempted from the provisions of section 29(1):
SCHEDULE
(a) All employees in Newspaper Printing Presses.
(b) All employees in Process factories, Iron and Steel, Aluminium, Copper and
Brass Rolling Mills.
(c) All employees in Tea Factories.
(d) Employees in public utility transport workshops (where this exemption is
considered necessary by the Chief Inspector-cum-Facilitator).
(e) Any other classes of employees in the auxiliary sections of large factories
where steam or electricity is generated or transformed for use in the
factory may be declared to be so exempted in writing by the Chief
Inspector-cum-Facilitator on application by the Manager through online or
electronically, in consideration of the essential or continuous nature of the
duties involved.
(f) Any special class of employees in any other factory were overlapping of
shifts is considered necessary by the Chief Inspector-cum-Facilitator.
(g) All employees in all factories to the extent of thirty minutes, where the
overlapping of shifts is intended to facilitate the smooth change-over of
shifts without interruption of work provided that both the groups of
employees do not carry out the same work at the same time.
29. Restriction of double employment under Section 30:-
An adult worker may be employed in more than one factory on the same
day if,-
(1) he is employed or engaged on a part-time basis to do any work; or
(2) he is employed or engaged to carry out any particular work of an
occasional nature otherwise than as a full-time employee; or
(3) the occupier or owner is unaware that the worker is employed on a full-
time basis in any other factory

30. Notice of periods of work under Section 31 (2):-
(1) The notice referred to in Section 31 shall be displayed at conspicuous
places on a notice board or electronic board in Telugu, English, and the
language understood by the majority of the employees in Form-VII, and a
copy of such notice shall be sent to the Inspector-cum-Facilitator
electronically .
(2) Any proposed change in the system of work in any establishment which
will necessitate a change in the notice referred to in sub-rule (1) shall be
intimated to the Inspector-cum-Facilitator before the change is made, and
except with the previous sanction of the Inspector-cum-Facilitator, no such
change shall be made until one week has elapsed since that last change.
(3) Any proposed change in the system of work in any establishment which
will necessitate a change in the notice referred in sub-rule (1) shall be
displayed at conspicuous places on a notice board or electronic board and
sent to the Inspector-cum-Facilitator electronically or by speed post in the
below pro-forma.
Name & Address of the Establishment:
Name & Designation of the Worker or Employee
Date of Work & Normal Period of Work
From which shift to another shift
Purpose of work (urgent work/ break down/
absence of shift reliever)
Signature of Manager/Person Authorised

31. Annual leave with wages under Section 32:
(1) Account of leave with wages: - The employer shall on a request from any
worker, forthwith provide him with an abstract of his account of leave
earned, leave allowed and the balance of leave standing to his credit for
the period not exceeding one year period to the date of such request.
(2) Medical Certificate: - If any worker is absent from work due to his/her
illness and he/she wants to avail himself/herself of the leave with wages
due to him/her to cover the period of illness as far as possible under the

provisions of the code shall, if so required by his/her manager by a notice
in writing, submit a medical certificate signed by a registered Medical
practitioner.
(3) Grant of leave with wages: -
(a) Whenever leave with wages is granted to any worker, necessary entries
shall be made in the leave with wages record.
(b) As far as circumstances permit, members of the same family shall be
allowed leave at the same time.
(c) A worker may exchange the period of his/her leave with another worker,
subject to the approval of the Manager
(4) Payment of wages if the worker dies: - If a worker dies, the cash
equivalent to the balance of leave in his/her credit shall be paid to his/her
nominee within one week of the receipt of intimation of the death of the
worker.
(5) Payment of wages if worker resigns: -if the worker resigns from the
establishment, the leave with wages due to him/her shall be paid at the
time of relieving the said worker from the establishment.
CHAPTER-VI
MAINTENANCE OF REGISTERS, RECORDS AND RETURNS
32. Maintenance and production of reports, registers and other records
under Section 33:
(1) The following registers, records, etc. shall be maintained electronically or
manually in an establishment and shall be produced or caused to be
produced, or by speed post as the case may be, on demand before the
Chief Inspector-cum-facilitator or an Inspector-cum-facilitator or any
person authorized in that behalf by the Government of Andhra Pradesh.
(a) Every employer or contractor subject to the provisions of this code, as the
case may be, shall maintain a register of employees of all the persons
employed by him in the factory/establishment in Form-VIII electronically
or otherwise and entries of attendance shall be made up to date at the
time of commencement of work in each day in Form-VIII(A) in
attendance register-cum-muster roll with the particulars like weekly day of
rest, substituted or compensatory holiday allowed, days of leave, overtime
hours worked etc., shall also be reflected in the record. The employer shall
make it readily available for inspection to the Inspector-cum-Facilitator at

all times during working hours.
(b) Every employer shall maintain a register of workers, wages, overtime,
and fine, deduction for damage or loss in Form-IX.
(c) Every employer shall maintain a record of leave with wages in electronic
form or otherwise in Form X.
(d) Every employer shall maintain the registers of accidents and dangerous
occurrences in Form-XI.
(e) A record of Overtime slips issued to each worker when the workers are
employed on overtime.
(f) Every employer shall maintain a register for the Adolescent workers
employed in Form- XXII
(g) Every employer shall maintain a record of overtime slips issued to each
worker when the workers are each time employed on overtime.
(h) Such records, as may be prescribed by the Chief Inspector-cum-
Facilitator, in respect of monitoring of the working environment in the
factory/establishment.
(2) In case of manual registers and other records, entry shall be made with
indelible ink in the language understood by a majority of the persons
employed;
(3) All reports, registers and other records shall be preserved in original for a
period of five (5) calendar years after the date of the last report or entry;
Provided that when the original record is lost or destroyed before the
expiry of five (5) years period, true copies thereof, if available, shall be
preserved for the prescribed period;
(4) Display of notice board:
(a) Every employer shall of the establishment cause to display at the
entrance of premises of establishment under his control, a prominent
board showing the name and address of the establishment and a notice at
the conspicuous place in the establishment showing hours of work, wage
period, date of payment of such wages, details of accident and dangerous
occurrence in the establishment for the last five years, name and address
of the Inspector-cum-Facilitator having jurisdiction to such establishment
in Telugu, English and in the language understood by the majority of the
workers.

(b) Every employer of the factory shall cause to be displayed at the entrance
of the premises of the establishment under his control, a prominent board
showing the following details:
Name and address of the Establishment. i)
ii) Registration Number of the Establishment.
iii) Licence Number of the Establishment.
iv) Licence Limits (Maximum Employment and Horse Power) of the
Establishment.
v) Wage period.
vi) Date of payment of such wages,
vii) The details of accidents and dangerous occurrences in the
establishment for the last five years.
viii) The name, address and telephone number of the Inspector-cum-
facilitator having jurisdiction to such establishment in Telugu, English
and in the Language understood by the majority of the workers.
(c) Every employer shall cause to display at workplaces respectivesafety
precautions, Standard Safe Operation Procedures, Safety Signages relating
to Personal Protective Equipment (PPE) and any other information related
to Safety, Health and welfare of the workers as suggested by the Inspector
Cum Facilitator.
(d) The above notices shall be displayed in Telugu and English or in a
language understood by the majority of workers/employees and shall be
maintained in clean and legible condition.

(5) Returns:
a. Every employer of an establishment shall submit annually a return relating
to such establishment in Form-XII to the Inspector-cum-Facilitator having
jurisdiction through the online portal as notified by the State Government,
so as to reach him not later than 1stFebruary following the end of each
calendar year, with a copy to the Director General, Labour Bureau
electronically.
b. Every contractor shall submit half-yearly returns in FORM-XII(A) to the
Inspector-cum-Facilitator having jurisdiction electronically through the

online portal as notified by the State Governmentnot more than thirty days
after completion of each half-year of the calendar.
CHAPTER-VII
INSPECTOR-CUM-FACILITATOR AND OTHER AUTHORITY
33. Appointment and qualifications of Chief Inspector-cum-Facilitator
and Inspector-cum-Facilitators under sub-section (1)and sub-
section (5) of Section 34: -
(1) Appointment of Chief Inspector-cum-Facilitator and Inspector-
cum-Facilitators:
(a) The State Government may, by notification, appoint a Gazetted officer of
that Government to be the Chief Inspector-cum-Facilitator under this code
who shall be responsible for effectively carrying out the provisions of the
Code in the State and shall also exercise the powers of anInspectors-cum-
Facilitator under this Code throughout the State in relation to the
establishments for which the State Government is the appropriate
Government.
(b) The appropriate Government may, by notification, appoint such number of
its officers as it thinks fit to be Inspectors-cum- Facilitators for the
purposes of this Code and may assign to them such local limits as it may
think fit.
(c) Every Inspectors-cum- Facilitators appointed under this rule shall be
subject to the control of the Chief Inspectors-cum- Facilitator and shall
exercise his powers and perform his functions under this Code subject to
the general control and supervision of the Chief-Inspectors-cum-
Facilitator.
(d) The State Government may, by notification, appoint for the purpose of
establishments as may be notified by the Government, as many and any
other officer of any designation as it thinks appropriate, to exercise such
powers of the Chief Inspector-cum-Facilitator within his jurisdiction, as
may be specified in the notification and also exercise the powers of an
Inspector-cum-Facilitator within such local limits.
(2) The qualification and experience of Chief Inspector-cum- Facilitator
for the purpose of Factories: The qualification of Chief Inspector-cum-
facilitator shall be as prescribed in Andhra Pradesh Factories Service Rules
for the post of Director of Factories in case of factories.
(3) Every Chief-Inspector-cum- Facilitator and every Inspector-cum-Facilitator

for establishments, factories and every other officer appointed under this
Code shall be deemed to be public servants within the meaning of Section
21 of the Indian Penal Code (45 of 1860).
34. Conducting of inspections and Inspection Scheme under sub-
section (3) of Section 34:
The State Government, by notification, lays down a comprehensive
procedure for conducting and generating web-based inspections and calling
of information under the Code electronically or as decided by the
government from time to time.
35. Power to take samples of any articles or substances under clause
(x) of sub-section (1) of Section 35.-
(1) An Inspector-cum-Facilitator shall take a sufficient sample of any
substance used or intended to be used in a factory, after informing its
employer, in the manner hereinafter provided, such use being–
(a) in the belief of the Inspector-cum-Facilitator in contravention of any of
the provisions of this code or the rules made thereunder, or
(b) in the opinion of the Inspector-cum-Facilitator likely to cause bodily
injury to, or injury to the health where the Inspector-cum-Facilitator takes
a sample under sub-rule (1), he shall, in the presence of the person
informed under that sub-section unless such person willfully absents
himself, divide the sample into three portions and effectively seal and
suitably mark them, and shall permit such person to add his own seal and
mark thereto.
(2) Where the Inspector-cum-facilitator takes a sample under sub-rule (1), he
shall, in the presence of the person informed under that sub-section unless
such person willfully absents himself, divide the sample into three portions
and effectively seal and suitably mark them, and shall permit such
personto add his own seal and mark thereto.
(3) The person informed as aforesaid shall, if the Inspector-cum-Facilitator so
requires, provide the appliance for dividing, sealing and marking the
sample taken under this section.
(4) The Inspector-cum-Facilitator shall-
(a) forthwith give one portion of the sample to the person informed under
sub-rule(1);
(b) forthwith send the second portion to a Government Analyst or National

Accreditation Board for Testing and Calibration Laboratories (NABL) for
analysis and report thereon;
(c) retain the third portion for production to the Court before which
proceedings, if any, are instituted in respect of the substance.
(5) Any document purporting to be a report under the hand of any
Government Analyst or National AccreditationBoard for Testing and
Calibration Laboratories (NABL) accredited laboratory upon any substance
submitted to him for analysis and report under this section, may be used
as evidence in any proceedings instituted in respect of the substance.
(6) The expenses for the collection of samples, testing and preserving of such
samples shall be incurred by the concerned establishment.

36. Powers and duties of Inspector-cum-Facilitator under clause (xiv) of
sub-section (1) of Section 35.-
(1) . If it appears to the Inspector-cum-Facilitator that, any building or part of
a building or any part of the ways, machinery or plant in a factory or any
building or other construction work is in such a condition that it is
dangerous to human life or safety or health, he may serve on to the
occupier of the factory or employer of the building or other construction
work, an order in writing specifying the measures which in his opinion
should be adopted and requiring them to be carried out before a specified
date.
(2) . An Inspector-cum-Facilitatorshall, at each inspection, ascertain to what
extent any short comings notified at a previous inspection have been
rectified and the notices previously issued have been complied with and his
findings and any short comings which may come to light during the
inspection, together with any order passed by him under the Code or the
regulations made there undershall be recorded and maintained.
(3) The Inspector-cum-Facilitator shall, after every inspection, as may be
deemed necessary, issue an improvement notice in Form-XII B pointing
out the non-compliance of provisions of safety, health and working
conditions under the Code, and rules and regulations framed thereunder,
to the employer of the establishment.
(4) The Inspector-cum-Facilitator shall exercise such other power and perform
such other duties as may be required by general or special order of the
State Government or Chief Inspector cum Facilitator.

37. Qualification, experience, duties and responsibilities of experts to
be empanelled for the purpose of third-party audit and /or
compliance certification:
The State Government may, by notification, formulate a scheme to
empanel the experts for the purpose of third-party audit and or compliance
certification under Section 37 of the code in respect of the factories as
stipulated under the code and rules.
Provided that such experts possess the prescribed qualification and
experience, and shall carry out the duties and responsibilities for the
purpose of third-party audit and or compliance certification as set out in
the scheme.
38. The manner of providing alternative employment under sub-clause
(d) of clause (A) of sub-section (1) of section 38.
(1) If employment of any worker or group of workers is affected due to an
order issued by an Inspector cum Facilitator under Sub-Section 1(A)(a) of
section 38 of the code, the occupier of the factory shall provide an
alternate employment without causing any reduction in the wages being
paid to the worker or workers immediately before issue of such an order.
The occupier shall, to the extent practicable, provide the said alternate
employment in a job matching the existing skill sets of the workers and, in
the absence of such an employment, any other job mutually acceptable to
the worker and occupier shall be arranged, provided that the safety and
health of the workers at work is not affected by such alternate
employment. In no case shall the workers be compelled to perform a job
that they consider not suitable or acceptable to them, and in all such
instances, the wages shall be paid in full, even without alternate
employment
Provided that the safety and health of the workers at work is not affected
by such alternate employment, and in no case, the workers shall be
compelled to perform a job which the workers consider not suitable or
acceptable to them and in all such instances, the wages shall be paid in full
even without alternate employment.
(2) The occupier of the factory, after having provided such alternate
employment, shall send a notice to the Chief inspector-cum-Facilitator
within 24 hours of provision of such employment.

39. Appointment of Medical officer under sub-section (1) & (2) of
Section 42:-
(1) The Medical Officer shall be a medical practitioner who possesses any
recognised medical qualification as defined in the National Medical
Commission Act, 2019(30 of 2019) and who is enrolled on the Indian
Medical Register as defined in clause (e) and on a State Medical Register as
defined in clause (l) of sections 35, 36, 37 and 40 of the said Act.
(2) The medical officer shall be trained in the Occupational Health course,
including the International LabourOrganisation (ILO) classification of chest
Radiograph duly recognised by the Central Government.
(3) Duties of Medical Officer:-
(a) On receipt of a reference under clause (c) sub section (2) of section 42 of
the code, the Medical Officer shall, after giving prior notice regarding date,
time and place for medical examination and upon examining the person
sent for such examination, prepare the age and fitness certificate and
deliver the same to the employer of the establishment concerned after
retaining a copy thereof.
(b) The medical officer may seek the opinion of specialists like radiologists,
dentist and orthopedic surgeons as the case may be, for the purpose of
determining age.
(c) Medical Officer shall carry out such examination and furnish such report as
theState Government may direct.
(i) for examination and certification of workers in an establishment in such
dangerous occupation or hazardous processes as specified in the First
Schedule to the Code;
(ii) for medical supervision of any establishment or class of establishment
where cases of chronic occupational illness have occurred due to the
arduous nature of any process carried on or hazardous condition of work;
(iii) in respect of any establishment or class of establishment or description of
establishment in which operations involve any risk of injury to the health of
any person or class of persons employed therein;
(iv) to undertake an occupational health survey for any class of an
establishment, where cases of illness have occurred, or there is prevalence
of diseases as prescribed in the Third Schedule of the code.
(v) to assess the age and issue fitness of an adolescent for employment in an

establishment or class of establishment.
CHAPTER-VIII
SPECIAL PROVISION RELATING TO EMPLOYMENT OF WOMEN
40. Employment of Women in establishments under Section 43:- The
following conditions shall be met for employment of women during night or
before 6.00 a.m. and beyond 7.00 p.m. on any day, namely: -
(1) The consent of women employee shall be taken in writing;
(2) No woman shall be employed against the maternity benefit provisions laid
down under the Social Security Code, 2020 (36 of 2020);
(3) adequate transportation facilities shall be provided to women employees to
pick up and drop off such employees at their residences;
(4) the workplace, including passage towards conveniences or facilities
concerning toilet, washrooms, drinking water, entry and exit of women
employees, should be well-lit;
(5) the toilet, washroom and drinking facilities should be near the workplace
where such women employees are employed, and suitable provisions of
Closed-circuit television (CCTV)surveillance on the way to these facilities
should be provided;
(6) Sanitary napkins of adequate quantity conforming to relevant Indian
Standards formulated by the Bureau of Indian Standards shall be provided
and maintained in the women’s toilets for their use, and the same shall be
replenished on a daily basis.
(7) Disposable bins with lids shall be provided within the women’s toilets for
the collection of the used sanitary napkins. The used sanitary napkins shall
be disposed of in a safe, hygienic and eco-friendly process by using a
(8) Provide safe, secure and healthy working conditions such that no woman
employee is disadvantaged in connection with her employment;
(9) Dedicated telephone numbers of the establishments shall be displayed at
conspicuous places both at the establishment as well as inside the vehicles,
so that any woman employee can utilise them, in case of any exigency or
emergency.
(10) The provisions of the Sexual Harassment of Women at Workplace

(Prevention, Prohibition and Redressal) Act, 2013 (ActNo.14 of 2013), as
applicable to the establishments, shall be complied with.
(11) Any other safety and security measures as may be notified by the state
government from time to time
41. Safety of employment of women under Section 44:
(1) No woman employee/worker shall be allowed or required to work near the
machinery in motion without wearing a head cap, apron and suitable
overcoat so as to avoid the risk of entanglement of clothes and bodily
injury from any moving part.
(2) Notwithstanding the provisions under clause (iv) of sub-section (1) of
section 24, suitable arrangements for sitting shall be provided and
maintained separately for all women workers obliged to work in the
standing position, in order that they may take advantage of any
opportunities for rest which may occur in the course of their work.
(3) As far as practicable woman worker shall not be engaged in any
workplace involving poor ergonomics detrimental to the safety and health.
(4) The employer shall ensure that the women are not allowed to be exposed
to materials that may influence or affect their menstrual cycles,
reproductive health or pregnancy.
(5) The State Government may declare by notification the class of
establishments and standards for safeguarding of women in hazardous and
dangerous processes from time to time.

CHAPTER-IX
CONTRACT LABOUR
42. Conditions of License under sub-section (3) of Section 47:
(1) The contractor shall ensure that:
(a) The hours of work shall conform to the rules made under Section 25 of
the Occupational Safety, Health and Working Conditions Code, 2020
(Act.No.37 of 2020).
(b) The wages shall be paid in accordance with the Code on Wages, 2019 (Act
No. 29 of 2020).

(2) The contractor shall inform within fifteen days of the receipt of a work
order, about the details of the contract and in the manner specified
under rule 55.
(3) (a) If a contract worker of the contractor is working at the premises of the
principal employer, then it shall be the responsibility of the principal
employer to provide the facilities or amenities such as a toilet, washroom,
drinking water, bathing facilities if required, changing room, first aid box,
canteen and crèche.
(b) All other facilities and entitlements other than those specified in clause (a)
shall be provided by the contractor
(4) In case the contractor fails to make payment of minimum wages to the
contract labour, the principal employer shall be liable to make payment of
the minimum wages to the concerned contract labour employed by the
contractor and recover the amount so paid from the contractor either by
deduction from any amount payable to the contractor under any contract
or as a debt payable by the contractor as specified at Section 55(3) of the
OSH Code.
(5) The contract labour shall also approach the authority notified under sub-
section (1) of Section 45 of the Code on Wages, 2019, who shall cause
such payment to be made to the contract worker.
43. The form and manner of application for the grant of a common or
single licence and the particulars that the application shall contain
regarding the number of contract labour/ inter-state migrant
workers, nature of work for which they are to be employed and
other particulars under sub-section (1) of Section 48:
(1) The contractor shall make an application for engaging contract workers/
inter-state migrant workers and for obtaining a common licence in respect
of a factory, industrial premises for beedi and cigar work and for engaging
contract workers or any combination thereof or a single licence in any one
of them.
(2) Every application by a contractor for the grant of a licence shall be made
online electronically through meeseva online portal or any other portal as
notified by the Government of Andhra Pradesh in Form-XIII to the
licensing authority, along with online payment of the appropriate licence
fee specified in Rule 45.
(3) Every application shall be accompanied by an undertaking in Form-XIV by
the contractor regarding non-engagement of contract labourers in the core
activity of the establishment.

(4) Every application for the grant of a license shall be accompanied by a
certificate from the principal employer in Form XV.
(5) Every application shall be accompanied by the Security Deposit as specified
in Rule 45.
44. Forms, terms and conditions of license: -
(1) Every license granted shall be in Form-XVI.
(2) Every license granted or renewed is subject to the following conditions,
namely:—
(a) The license shall be non-transferable, except in the case of the death of
the contractor.
(b) The number of workers employed as contract labour by the contractor shall
not, on any day, exceed the maximum number specified in the license;
(c) save as provided in these rules, the fees paid for the grant, or as the case
may be, for renewal of the license shall be non-refundable;
(d) The rates of wages payable to the workers by the contractor shall not be
less than the rates prescribed under the Code on Wages, 2019, and where
the rates have been fixed by agreement, settlement or award, it shall not
be less than the rates so fixed in the Code on Wages, 2019.
45. Procedure for issue of license under sub-section (2) of Section 48.-
(1) Before a license is issued, security deposit calculated at the rate of one
thousand rupees (Rs.1000/-) for each of the workmen to be employed as
contract labour or inter-state migrant worker in respect of which the
application for license has been made, shall be deposited by the contractor
for due performance of the conditions of the license and compliance with
the provisions of the Code or the rules made there under.
(2) The amount of security deposit to be deposited under sub-rule (1) shall be
paid through meeseva online portal or any other portal as notified by the
state government. The state government shall, by notification, prescribe
the procedure of deposit, holding and maintenance of the security deposit.
(3) Where a contractor's license has expired and when he wishes to submit an
application for another new license, he may request the Licensing Authority
to adjust the security deposit related to the expired license against the new
license and the Licensing Authority may, at its discretion, consider such a
request and adjust the security deposit accordingly.

(4) The fees to be paid for the grant of a license shall be as specified in the
table below, namely;

No. of Contract Labour Fees in Rs.
No license is required up to 49 Nil
50 to 100 5,000/-
101 to 200 10,000/-
201to 300 15,000/-
301to 400 20,000/-
401 to 500 25,000/-
501 to 600 30,000/-
601 to 1000 40,000/-
Above 1000 50,000/-

Provided that the above License fee shall be as modified by means of a
notification issued by the Government from time to time.
(5) License issued under this rule shall be valid for one or three financial years,
as the case may be.
46. Renewal of license under section 48.-
(1) Every contractor shall apply electronically to the appropriate licensing
authority for renewal of the license in Form-XIII through meeseva online
portal or any other portal as notified by the Government of Andhra
Pradesh.
(2) Every such application shall be submitted on the said portal at least 30
days prior to expiry of the license period, but not before 90 days of such
expiry of the license.
(3) The security deposit and the fee chargeable for renewal of the license shall
be the same as for the grant of a license under rule 45.
Provided that if the application for renewal is not received within the time
specified in sub- rule (2), an additional fee of twenty five percent shall be

payable for such renewal.
(4) It shall be the responsibility of the authority concerned to renew the
license within seven (7) days electronically, failing which the license shall
be deemed to be issued and auto-generated and the responsibility of such
failure shall be on such authority.

47. Refund of security deposit:-
(1) On expiry of the period of license, the contractor may, if he does not
intend to have his license renewed further, make an application
electronically to the licensing authority for the refund of the security
deposit deposited by him along with the license and notice of completion of
work.
(2) If the Licensing authority is satisfied that there is no breach of the
conditions of the license or any provision of the OSH Code or these Rules
or there is no order for the forfeiture of the security deposit or any portion
thereof, he shall direct the refund of the security deposit to the applicant.
(3) If there is any order directing the forfeiture of any portion of the
contractor’s security deposit, the amount to be forfeited shall be deducted
from the security deposit, and the balance, if any, shall be refunded to the
contractor.
(4) Any application for refund shall, as far as possible, be disposed of within
30 days of the receipt of the application.
48. Responsibility of contractor under sub-section (4) of section 48.-
(1) The rates of wages payable to the workers by the contractor shall not be
less than the rates prescribed under the Code on Wages, 2019 and where
the rates have been fixed by agreement, settlement or award, not less
than the rates so fixed.
(2) In case where the worker employed by the contractor perform the same or
similar kind of work as the worker directly employed by the principal
employer of the establishment, the wage rates, holidays, hours of work
and other conditions of service of the workers of the contractor shall be the
same as applicable to the workers directly employed by the principal
employer of the establishment on the same or similar kind of work. In case
of any dispute whether the work is of a similar kind, the matter shall be
referred to the Chief Inspector-Cum Facilitator or the Inspector-cum-
Facilitator as notified by the Government and whose decision shall be final.

(3) In other cases, the wage rates, holidays, hours of work and conditions of
service of the workers of the contractor shall be such as specified under
the Code, and rules made thereunder.
(4) All contract labour shall be made members of Employee’s Provident Fund
organisation (EPFO) and Employee State Insurance Scheme (ESIC),
subject to applicability as under respective provisions of the Code on Social
Security, 2020.
(5) The contractor shall notify in Form XIII any change in the number of
workers or conditions of work to the Licensing Authority, electronically.
49. Intimation of work order and time limit for intimation under
Section 50:-
(1) Every contractor shall within fifteen days of the receipt of a contract work
order shall intimate to the authority about the contract work order
containing the details such as the name of the principal employer, address
of the premises where work is being undertaken, date of commencement
of the contract work, the number of contract labour employed under that
work order, duration of work orders.
(2) The details of the work order shall be sent by the contractor or his
authorized representative.
(3) The intimation shall be sent electronically on the Online Portal of the
Labour Department or e-mail of the Chief Inspector-cum-Facilitator or
Licensing authority as may be notified on the website of the Labour
Department.
50. Revocation or suspension of license under Section 50(2) &51:-
(1) If the Licensing Authority is satisfied that the License has been obtained
by mis-representation or suppression of any material fact or if the
contractor has failed to comply with the conditions subject to which License
was granted or the contractor has contravened any provision of the Part-I,
Chapter XI of the Code or rules made there under, the Licensing Authority
shall issue a show cause notice of fifteen (15) days to the contractor
electronically. On receipt of the reply if any, from the contractor within
fifteen (15) days, the Licensing Authority shall examine the same and in
case the licensing authority feels that the continuation of contract business
by the contractor is going to lead to grave harm to the workers, he may
pass a Speaking Order recording the reasons for revocation or suspension
or otherwise and communicate to the contractor electronically.
(2) If the contractor has complied with the said provisions of the code and

rules made thereunder within the stipulated time period, the Licensing
Authority shall revoke the suspension, giving a Speaking Order or else the
suspension may be continued.
(3) If the contractor fails to comply with the directions as in sub-rule (1), the
Licensing Authority may forthwith pass an order of revocation of license,
recording the reasons thereof and communicate to the contractor
electronically.

51. Amendment of license under sub-section 2 of Section 51:-
(1) Every contractor shall apply electronically to the appropriate licensing
authority for the amendment of the license in Form-XIII, along with
payment of the appropriate fee and security deposit for such amendment
through meeseva online portal or any other portal as notified by the
Government of Andhra Pradesh.
(2) Every such application shall be submitted on the online portal within thirty
days from the date of such amendment required in the license.
(3) The fee for the amendment of the license shall be five hundred rupees plus
the amount that would have been payable if the license had originally been
issued in the amended form, deducting the fees originally paid for the
license.
(4) The additional amount of security deposit shall be at the rate of Rs.1000/-
(one thousand rupees only) for every worker.
52. The form of application, manner of filing the application for a single
license for a contractor in morethan one district or for the whole of
Andhra Pradesh:-
(1) If a contractor desirous of obtaining a license for:-
(a) supplying or engaging contract labour/ inter-state migrant workers or
(b) undertaking or executing the contract works under sub-section (1) or
sub-section (2) of section 47 in more than one district or for the whole of
Andhra Pradesh, then he shall apply electronically through meeseva online
portal or any other portal as notified by the Government of Andhra Pradesh
in Form-XIII to the licensing authority as notified by the State
Government.
(2) Application for a single license shall be submitted electronically to the

authority notified in this behalf under sub-section (1) of Section 119 of this
code.
(3) The concerned districts shall be consulted electronically. If no response is
received within thirty days from the date of issue of such communication,
the consultation process is deemed to have been complied with.
(4) All the provisions of rules 45 to 50 shall apply mutatis mutandis in case of
the single license granted under sub-rule (1).
(5) License issued under this rule shall be valid for one or three financial
years, as the case may be.
(6) Copy or copies of the license so issued shall be sent to the Licensing
authorities in the districts, in whose jurisdiction the contract work or works
for which the license is issued fall.
53. Procedure for transfer of licensee in case of death of licensee:
(1) If a Licensee dies, his heir or legal representative shall, within two months
from the date of the death of the Licensee, make an application to the
licensing authority in Form XVIIfor the transfer of the Licence in his
name.
(2) The licensing authority shall, if it approves of the transfer, enter the license
under its digital signature and endorsement to the effect that the license
has been transferred to the applicant.
(3) An application for the transfer of a License under this rule shall be dealt
with by the licensing authority in the same manner as an application for
the grant of a License.
54. Appeal under sub-section (1) of Section 52:-The appellate authority
under sub-section (1) of Section 52 of the Code shall be an authority
notified by the State Government.
55. Responsibility of Payment of wages under Section 55:
(1) The contractor shall fix the wage periods in respect of which wages shall be
payable, and no wage period shall exceed one (1) month.
(2) The wages of every person employed as contract labour in an
establishment or by a contractor shall be paid before the expiry of the
seventh day after the last day of the wage period in respect of which the
wages are payable.

(3) The wages shall be disbursed through bank transfer or electronic mode
only.
56. Making payment of wages from the security deposit amount under
sub-section (4) of Section 55:-
If the contractor or principal employer does not pay the wages to the
contract labour employed by him, the Chief Inspector-cum-facilitator or the
competent officer as may be notified by the State Government shall
conduct or cause to conduct, an inquiry and after giving an opportunity to
be heard to both the principal employer and the contractor and shall pass
an order to make payment if any, of such wages, from the amount
deposited by the contractor as security deposit. The contractor shall re-
furnish the security deposit within a period of fifteen days, or else his
license will be liable to be suspended. In case of contractor fails to make
payment of wages within the prescribed period or makes short payment,
then the principal employer shall be liable to make payment of wages in
full or the unpaid balance due, as the case may be, to the contract labour
employed by the contractor and recover the amount so paid from any
amount payable to the contractor under any contract or as a debt payable
by the contractor.
57. Experience Certificate under Section 56:-
Every concerned contractor shall issue on demand, experience
certificate in Form-XVIII to the contract labour giving details of the
period, work performed, experience gained in various fields performed by
such contract labour under him.
58. Prohibition of employment of contract labour under clause (b) of
sub-section (2) of Section 57:-
If a question arises as to whether any activity of an establishment is a core
activity or otherwise, the aggrieved party may make an application to the
authority notified by the Government of Andhra Pradesh, giving reasons
along with supporting documents.
59. Period of making report and the period of deciding the question
under clause (c) of sub-section (2) of Section 57:-
The State Governmenton receiving the application on the question whether
any activity of an establishment is a core activity or otherwiseshall, if deem
proper, refer the application, to the designated authority for making such
enquiry as may be required and the designated authority shall submit the
report/ recommendation to the Government for taking appropriate action
under these rules and the Government shall decide the matter within thirty

days from the date of receipt of such report/recommendationand
communicate the said decision to the parties.
CHAPTER-X
INTER-STATE MIGRANT WORKER
60. Journey allowance to an Inter-State Migrant Worker under Section
61: -
(1) The employer shall pay a lump sum amount on account of fare for
to and fro journey to inter-state migrant worker by train (not less
than II Class Sleeper) or by bus or any other mode of passenger
transport from the place of employment to the place of residence in
the home- state, if he has worked for a period of not less than 180
days in the concerned establishment(s) in preceding twelve
months;Provided that the journey allowance shall be given to an
inter-state migrant worker once in twelve months.
(2) In the event of change of employer by the inter-state migrant
worker during the middle of the employment period and has not
availed the journey allowance from his previous employer, then on
the basis of a certificate to be given by inter-state migrant worker,
the employer where the inter-state migrant worker is now working
and the such worker has completed one hundred and eighty days in
preceding twelve months including the period spent with the previous
employer, then the employer shall give journey allowance.
61. Setting up of a Toll-Free helpline number to the inter-state migrant
worker under Section 63:-A Toll-Free helpline number shall be provided
by the Chief Inspector-cum-Facilitator/Commissioner of Labour,
Government of Andhra Pradesh, to address queries and grievances of the
inter-state migrant workers.
62. Study of inter-state migrant workers under Section 64: -The
Government of Andhra Pradesh may identify the studies to be carried out
to promote the safety, health and welfare of inter-state migrant workers.
Wherever required, the State Government may also consult theexpert
organisations and different stakeholders involved in the safety, health and
welfare of inter-state migrant workers.
CHAPTER-XI
AUDIO-VISUAL WORKER

63. Agreement for audio-visual workers under clause (a) of sub-section (2) of
section 66:-
(1) The form of agreement for the audio-visual worker with the producer is
given in Form XIX.
(2) The agreement shall be registered with the competent authority as may be
notified by the State Government.
(3) A copy of the agreement shall also be uploaded on the labour department
portal or any other method to be prescribed by the State Government.
64. Procedure for reference of disputes to a conciliation officer or a
tribunal under sub-section. (1) of Section 66:-
The procedure for reference of the dispute to a conciliation officer or
tribunal shall be in conformity with the Industrial Relations Code 2020, and
rules framed thereunder.

CHAPTER- XII
BEEDI AND CIGAR WORKERS
65. Form of application for grant of Licence, renewal, fee under sub-
section (1), (2), (3), (4) and (6) of Section 74:
(1) Every application under sub-section (1) of section 74 for a Licence to use
or allow to be used any place or premises as an industrial premise for
Beedi and Cigar establishments shall be made electronically in Form XIII
to the notified licencing authority under Section 119(1) of the Code
through meeseva online portal or any other portal as notified by the
Government of Andhra Pradesh.
(2) The applicant shall fill out the application and upload the following
documents, namely:-
(a) Plans, drawn to scaleshowing: -
i. The site of such place or premises, the areas therein to be used for
manufacturing processes and the immediate surroundings of such
place or premises, including adjacent buildings, structures, roads,
drains and the like; and

ii. the plan, elevation and necessary cross-sections of the details relating
to natural lighting, ventilation, means of escape in case of fire,
position of the plant and machinery, if any, used aisles and passage
ways in or in relation to the various buildings which are intended to be
used for manufacturing processes; and
(b) Online payment of appropriate fees for the Licence as specified in sub-rule
(6).
(3) Before granting a Licence, the competent authority shall also take into
consideration whether the site of any industrial premises is proposed to be
altered or whether any industrial premises is proposed to be altered or
whether any industrial premises has been closed, by the applicant during
the period of twelve months immediately preceding the date of the
application with a view to causing prejudice to the interests of the labour.
(4) The Licencing Authority shall grant the Licence electronically within forty-
five days of the receipt of the application, failing which the license shall be
deemed to be issued and shall be auto-generated, and the responsibility of
such failure shall be on such authority.
(5) Form and terms and conditions of licence:-
(a) A License granted under the Code shall be in Form XVI.
(b) The terms and conditions subject to which such License may be granted
or renewed shall be the following, namely:-
(i) The manufacturing process shall be carried on only in that part of
the premises specified in the License
(ii) The total number of employees employed in the premises shall
not, on any day, during the period of the License, exceed that
specified in the License.
(iii) No machinery or power which has not been specified in the License
shall be used in the manufacturing process in the premises.
(iv) Except with prior written permission of the licensing authority, the
industrial permission shall not be extended, and except with the
like permission, no structural alterations shall be made in any
building on such premises;
(v) The License shall not be transferable from one employer to another
except in the cases and in accordance with the procedure specified
in rule 53.

(vi) The fees paid shall not be refunded except in the cases specified in
rule 68.
(vii) The competent authority shall, before granting the license, satisfy
itself that new branches were opened by management by way of
only to expand business, and shall take into account the history of
such management closing and opening business in that area.
(viii) The licensing authority may, in addition to the foregoing terms and
conditions, determine and specify in the License such other terms
and conditions, in individual cases, as it may deem necessary.
(ix) The license granted shall be valid for five years and may be
renewed thereafter.
(6) Fees: - (a).The fees to be paid for the grant or renewal of a Licence under
Section 74 shall be as specified in the Table below:-
